                        "range rules were found."
                    )

                elif lower_value == upper_value:
                    if not (
                        lower_inclusive
                        and upper_inclusive
                    ):
                        errors.append(
                            f"{path}: Contradictory {attribute} "
                            "range rules were found."
                        )

    # Recursively inspect child groups
    for child_index, child_group in enumerate(
        group.child_groups,
        start=1,
    ):
        child_path = (
            f"{path}, Child Group {child_index}"
        )

        validate_numeric_rule_contradictions(
            group=child_group,
            path=child_path,
            errors=errors,
        )


def validate_dob_rule_contradictions(
    group: EligibilityRuleGroupData,
    path: str,
    errors: list[str],
):
    """
    Detect impossible Date of Birth combinations
    inside an AND rule group.

    Examples:

        Date of Birth >= 2020-01-01
        Date of Birth <= 1990-01-01

    or:

        Date of Birth = 2000-01-01
        Date of Birth < 1990-01-01
    """

    dob_rules = []

    for rule in group.rules:
        if rule.attribute != "Date of Birth":
            continue

        try:
            dob_value = date.fromisoformat(rule.value)

            if dob_value > date.today():
                continue

            dob_rules.append(
                (rule, dob_value)
            )

        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            continue

    # DOB contradiction checking only applies to AND groups.
    if group.logical_operator == "AND":

        lower_bounds = []
        upper_bounds = []
        exact_values = []

        for rule, value in dob_rules:

            if rule.operator == ">":
                lower_bounds.append(
                    (value, False)
                )

            elif rule.operator == ">=":
                lower_bounds.append(
                    (value, True)
                )

            elif rule.operator == "<":
                upper_bounds.append(
                    (value, False)
                )

            elif rule.operator == "<=":
                upper_bounds.append(
                    (value, True)
                )

            elif rule.operator == "=":
                exact_values.append(value)

        # Check conflicting exact DOB values
        if exact_values:

            if len(set(exact_values)) > 1:
                errors.append(
                    f"{path}: Contradictory Date of Birth "
                    "equality rules were found."
                )

            else:
                exact_value = exact_values[0]

                # Equality vs lower bound
                for lower_value, lower_inclusive in lower_bounds:
                    if (
                        exact_value < lower_value
                        or (
                            exact_value == lower_value
                            and not lower_inclusive
                        )
                    ):
                        errors.append(
                            f"{path}: Date of Birth equality "
                            "rule conflicts with a "
                            "lower-bound rule."
                        )

                # Equality vs upper bound
                for upper_value, upper_inclusive in upper_bounds:
                    if (
                        exact_value > upper_value
                        or (
                            exact_value == upper_value
                            and not upper_inclusive
                        )
                    ):
                        errors.append(
                            f"{path}: Date of Birth equality "
                            "rule conflicts with an "
                            "upper-bound rule."
                        )

        # Check lower-bound / upper-bound conflicts
        if lower_bounds and upper_bounds:

            strongest_lower = max(
                lower_bounds,
                key=lambda item: (
                    item[0],
                    item[1],
                ),
            )

            strongest_upper = min(
                upper_bounds,
                key=lambda item: (
                    item[0],
                    not item[1],
                ),
            )

            lower_value, lower_inclusive = strongest_lower
            upper_value, upper_inclusive = strongest_upper

            if lower_value > upper_value:
                errors.append(
                    f"{path}: Contradictory Date of Birth "
                    "range rules were found."
                )

            elif lower_value == upper_value:
                if not (
                    lower_inclusive
                    and upper_inclusive
                ):
                    errors.append(
                        f"{path}: Contradictory Date of Birth "
                        "range rules were found."
                    )

    # Recursively inspect child groups
    for child_index, child_group in enumerate(
        group.child_groups,
        start=1,
    ):
        child_path = (
            f"{path}, Child Group {child_index}"
        )

        validate_dob_rule_contradictions(
            group=child_group,
            path=child_path,
            errors=errors,
        )


def validate_eligibility_rules(
    data: EligibilityRulesData,
) -> list[str]:

    errors = []

    # At least one rule group is required
    if not data.rule_groups:
        errors.append(
            "Eligibility rules must contain at least "
            "one rule group."
        )

    # Validate each rule group
    for group_index, group in enumerate(
        data.rule_groups,
        start=1,
    ):
        group_path = f"Group {group_index}"

        # Structural validation
        validate_rule_group(
            group=group,
            path=group_path,
            errors=errors,
        )

        # Numeric contradiction validation
        validate_numeric_rule_contradictions(
            group=group,
            path=group_path,
            errors=errors,
        )

        # Date-of-birth contradiction validation
        validate_dob_rule_contradictions(
            group=group,
            path=group_path,
            errors=errors,
        )

    return errors

========== FILE: app\ai\extraction\age_rules.py ==========

from __future__ import annotations

"""
Deterministic age / date-of-birth helpers.

Pure Python (no pydantic, no database), so every function here can be
unit-tested on its own.

Why this module exists
----------------------

Notices word age limits in two different ways:

    "must not exceed 32 years"            -> age <= 32  (inclusive)
    "must not have attained 32 years"     -> age <= 31  (exclusive)

The LLM copies the number (32) in both cases. Treating both as
"age <= 32" gives a date-of-birth boundary that is exactly one year too
generous for the second wording (UPSC uses it). The wording is therefore
read from the quoted sentence, deterministically.

Many notices also state the birth-date window outright:

    "born not earlier than 2nd August, 1993 and not later than
     1st August, 2004"

When that sentence exists it is the authority, so it is parsed here
instead of being recomputed from an age.
"""

import re
from datetime import date, timedelta

from app.ai.extraction.dates import find_dates


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Date arithmetic
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def subtract_years(d: date, years: int) -> date:
    try:
        return d.replace(year=d.year - years)
    except ValueError:
        # 29 February
        return d.replace(year=d.year - years, day=28)


def dob_boundary(
    kind: str,
    years: int,
    reference: date,
    *,
    exclusive: bool = False,
) -> tuple[str, date]:
    """
    Convert an age limit into a date-of-birth rule.

    Returns (operator, date) for the rule "Date of Birth <operator> date".

    kind="maximum", exclusive=False  ->  age <= N  on `reference`
        born on or after (reference - (N+1) years) + 1 day

    kind="maximum", exclusive=True   ->  age <  N  on `reference`
        born on or after (reference - N years) + 1 day

    kind="minimum", exclusive=False  ->  age >= N  on `reference`
        born on or before (reference - N years)

    kind="minimum", exclusive=True   ->  age >  N  on `reference`
        born on or before (reference - (N+1) years)
    """

    if kind == "maximum":
        effective = years - 1 if exclusive else years
        boundary = subtract_years(reference, effective + 1) + timedelta(days=1)
        return ">=", boundary

    effective = years + 1 if exclusive else years
    return "<=", subtract_years(reference, effective)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Inclusive / exclusive wording
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_MAX_EXCLUSIVE = re.compile(
    r"""
    \b(?:
        (?:must\s+|should\s+|shall\s+)?not\s+(?:have\s+|yet\s+)?
            (?:attained|completed|reached)
      | (?:have|has)\s+not\s+(?:yet\s+)?(?:attained|completed|reached)
      | below\s+(?:the\s+age\s+of\s+)?\d
      | less\s+than\s+(?:the\s+age\s+of\s+)?\d
      | under\s+(?:the\s+age\s+of\s+)?\d
      | younger\s+than\s+\d
    )
    """,
    re.IGNORECASE | re.VERBOSE,
)

_MIN_EXCLUSIVE = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:more\s+than|older\s+than|above|over)\s+(?:the\s+age\s+of\s+)?\d",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


def is_exclusive(kind: str, quote: str | None) -> bool:
    """True when the quoted wording excludes the stated age itself."""

    if not quote:
        return False

    pattern = _MAX_EXCLUSIVE if kind == "maximum" else _MIN_EXCLUSIVE

    return bool(pattern.search(quote))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Explicit date-of-birth windows
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_EARLIEST = re.compile(
    r"(?:born\s+)?(?:not\s+earlier\s+than|on\s+or\s+after|not\s+before)\s*",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

_LATEST = re.compile(
    r"(?:not\s+later\s+than|on\s+or\s+before|not\s+after)\s*",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

_BORN = re.compile(r"\bborn\b", re.IGNORECASE)


def _first_date_after(text: str, position: int, reach: int = 45) -> date | None:
    window = text[position : position + reach]

    mentions = sorted(find_dates(window), key=lambda m: m.start)

    return mentions[0].value if mentions else None


def parse_explicit_dob_limits(
    text: str,
    exclude: re.Pattern[str] | None = None,
) -> dict:
    """
    Find birth-date limits written out in the notice.

    Returns
        {"earliest": (date, snippet) | None,
         "latest":   (date, snippet) | None,
         "varies":   bool}

    Only text that follows the word "born" is considered, so sentences
    such as "applications not later than 11.02.2025" are never mistaken
    for a birth-date limit.

    Some notices give different limits for different posts ("posts with an
    age limit up to 30 years: born not earlier than 02.08.1996 ...; posts
    up to 32 years: born not earlier than 02.08.1994 ..."). A candidate who
    is eligible for any post is eligible for the exam, so the most generous
    limit is returned and `varies` is set so the reviewer is told.

    `exclude` is a pattern (category words such as SC/ST/ex-servicemen);
    a "born ..." sentence that starts with one of those words describes a
    concession, not the general limit, and is skipped.
    """

    earliest: list[tuple[date, str]] = []
    latest: list[tuple[date, str]] = []

    for born in _BORN.finditer(text):
        start = born.start()

        if exclude is not None:
            lead = re.split(r"[.\n;]", text[max(0, start - 80) : start])[-1]

            if exclude.search(lead):
                continue

        window = text[start : start + 260]

        for match in _EARLIEST.finditer(window):
            found = _first_date_after(window, match.end())

            if found:
                snippet = window[: min(len(window), match.end() + 45)]
                earliest.append((found, " ".join(snippet.split())))

        for match in _LATEST.finditer(window):
            found = _first_date_after(window, match.end())

            if found:
                snippet = window[: min(len(window), match.end() + 45)]
                latest.append((found, " ".join(snippet.split())))

    varies = (
        len({d for d, _ in earliest}) > 1
        or len({d for d, _ in latest}) > 1
    )

    return {
        "earliest": min(earliest, key=lambda item: item[0]) if earliest else None,
        "latest": max(latest, key=lambda item: item[0]) if latest else None,
        "varies": varies,
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# "as on" reference date
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_AGE_SENTENCE = re.compile(r"\bage\b|\bborn\b|\byears\b", re.IGNORECASE)

_AS_ON = re.compile(
    r"(?:as\s+on|as\s+of|reckoned\s+(?:as\s+)?on|calculated\s+(?:as\s+)?on|"
    r"counted\s+(?:as\s+)?on|on\s+the)\s*",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


def find_age_reference_date(text: str) -> date | None:
    """
    The single date on which age is counted, when the notice states it.

    Returns None unless exactly one distinct "as on <date>" date occurs in
    sentences that talk about age, so an ambiguous document never gets a
    guessed reference date.
    """

    candidates: set[date] = set()

    for line in re.split(r"(?<=[.;])\s+|\n", text):
        if not _AGE_SENTENCE.search(line):
            continue

        for match in _AS_ON.finditer(line):
            found = _first_date_after(line, match.end(), reach=32)

            if found:
                candidates.add(found)

    return next(iter(candidates)) if len(candidates) == 1 else None


========== FILE: app\ai\extraction\compiler.py ==========

from __future__ import annotations

"""
Facts -> the structures NexStep stores.

Everything here is deterministic: age limits become date-of-birth
boundaries, accepted qualifications become an OR group, and so on.

Important grounding rule:

- Informational facts may remain visible as warnings when they cannot
  be verified.
- Executable/authoritative rules must only be created when their
  supporting evidence can actually be located in the source document.
"""

import re
from datetime import date, timedelta

from app.ai.aggregated_extraction_result import ConflictOption
from app.ai.eligibility_rule_normalizer import normalize_date as normalize_any_date
from app.ai.eligibility_schemas import (
    EligibilityRuleData,
    EligibilityRuleGroupData,
    EligibilityRulesData,
)
from app.ai.exam_schemas import EligibilityInformation, ExamDateRange
from app.ai.extraction.facts import (
    AgeLimit,
    DatesDraft,
    EligibilityDraft,
    Fact,
)
from app.ai.extraction.age_rules import (
    dob_boundary,
    find_age_reference_date,
    is_exclusive,
    parse_explicit_dob_limits,
    subtract_years,  # re-exported: other modules and tests import it from here
)
from app.ai.extraction.grounding import Grounder, locate_quote


_LEVEL_ORDER = [
    "10th",
    "12th / Senior Secondary",
    "Diploma",
    "Bachelor's",
    "Master's",
    "PhD",
]


_CATEGORY_WORDS = re.compile(
    r"\b(obc|sc|st|ews|pwd|pwbd|ph|women|female|male|reserved|categor\w*|"
    r"ex-?servicem[ae]n|servicem[ae]n|relaxation|relaxable|domicile|defence|"
    r"handicapped|disab\w*|scheduled|castes?|tribes?|backward|benchmark\w*|"
    r"widows?|divorced|government\s+servants?|departmental)\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

# Wording that introduces a concession on top of the general limit
# ("up to a maximum of five years in the case of ...").
_RELAXATION_WORDS = re.compile(
    r"\b(relax\w*|up\s+to\s+a\s+maximum\s+of|in\s+the\s+case\s+of|"
    r"(?:three|five|ten|\d+)\s+years?\s+(?:if|for|in))\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

_GENERAL_WORDS = re.compile(
    r"\b(all|general|unreserved|ur|everyone|any|open)\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

_COUNTRY_PREFIX = re.compile(
    r"^(?:citizens?|subjects?|nationals?)\s+of\s+(?:the\s+)?",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

# A "country" that is really a sentence cannot be compared with a
# student's nationality.
_FREE_TEXT_COUNTRY = re.compile(
    r"[(),;]|\borigin\b|\brefugee\w*\b|\bmigrat\w*\b|\bwho\b|\bbefore\b|"
    r"\bafter\b|\bpermanent\w*\b|\bpersons?\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

# Wording that identifies each accepted educational level. Used to check a
# level against its quote ("Bachelor's" is backed by "a degree of a
# recognised university") and to find a quote when the model gave none.
_LEVEL_PATTERNS = {
    "10th": re.compile(
        r"\b(?:10th|tenth|matric\w*|class\s*(?:x|10)\b|high\s+school)",
        re.IGNORECASE,
    ),
    "12th / Senior Secondary": re.compile(
        r"(?:\b(?:12th|twelfth|10\s*\+\s*2|senior\s+secondary|"
        r"higher\s+secondary|intermediate|hsc|class\s*(?:xii|12)\b)|\+\s*2\b)",
        re.IGNORECASE,
    ),
    "Diploma": re.compile(r"\bdiploma\b", re.IGNORECASE),
    "Bachelor's": re.compile(
        r"\b(?:bachelor\w*|graduat\w*|undergraduate|degree|btech|bsc|bca|"
        r"b\.\s?(?:tech|e|sc|a|com|arch)\b)",
        re.IGNORECASE,
    ),
    "Master's": re.compile(
        r"\b(?:master\w*|post\s*-?\s*graduat\w*|mtech|msc|mca|mba|"
        r"m\.\s?(?:tech|e|sc|a)\b)",
        re.IGNORECASE,
    ),
    "PhD": re.compile(r"\b(?:ph\.?\s?d|doctor\w*)", re.IGNORECASE),
}

_BACHELOR_SPECIFIC = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:bachelor\w*|undergraduate|btech|bsc|bca|b\.\s?(?:tech|e|sc|a|com|arch)\b)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

_HIGHER_WORDS = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:master\w*|post\s*-?\s*graduat\w*|doctor\w*|ph\.?\s?d)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

_NATIONALITY_SENTENCE = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:citizen|national|subject)s?\b.{0,60}"
    r"\b(?:india|nepal|bhutan|bangladesh|sri\s+lanka)\b"
    r"|\bindian\s+(?:citizen|national)s?\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

# "... or (d) a person of Indian origin who has migrated from Pakistan,
# Burma, Sri Lanka ..." -- the countries after this phrase are where such
# people came from, not nationalities that may apply.
_ORIGIN_START = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:persons?\s+of\s+indian\s+origin|indian\s+origin|"
    r"migrated\s+from|came\s+over\s+from)\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

_PASSAGE_TAG = re.compile(r"^\[(P\d+) \| [^\n]*\]\s*$", re.MULTILINE)

_NUMBER_WORDS = {
    "one": "1", "two": "2", "three": "3", "four": "4", "five": "5",
    "six": "6", "seven": "7", "eight": "8", "nine": "9", "ten": "10",
    "eleven": "11", "twelve": "12", "fifteen": "15", "twenty": "20",
    "thirty": "30",
}


_DEMONYMS = {
    "indian": "India",
    "nepali": "Nepal",
    "nepalese": "Nepal",
    "bhutanese": "Bhutan",
    "bangladeshi": "Bangladesh",
    "sri lankan": "Sri Lanka",
    "pakistani": "Pakistan",
    "american": "United States",
    "british": "United Kingdom",
}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Small helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def iso(value: str | None) -> date | None:
    if not value:
        return None

    try:
        return date.fromisoformat(
            normalize_any_date(value.strip())
        )
    except Exception:
        return None


# subtract_years now lives in age_rules.py (imported above) so that the
# date arithmetic can be unit-tested without pydantic or a database.


def canonical_country(name: str) -> str:
    cleaned = _COUNTRY_PREFIX.sub("", name.strip().strip("."))

    return _DEMONYMS.get(
        cleaned.lower(),
        cleaned,
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Exam dates
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compile_dates(
    draft: DatesDraft,
    g: Grounder,
    tba_hint: bool,
) -> dict:
    """
    Validate and ground every date.

    A date is stored only when:
      1. it parses correctly,
      2. it occurs somewhere in the document,
      3. its supporting quote can be located, and
      4. the quote is compatible with the extracted date.

    This prevents an LLM-generated date from becoming authoritative
    merely because the date happens to appear somewhere in the PDF.
    """

    def scalar(
        fact: Fact,
        label: str,
    ) -> str | None:
        if not fact.value:
            return None

        parsed = iso(fact.value)

        if parsed is None:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-unparsed",
                f"{label}: '{fact.value}' is not a valid date.",
                label,
            )
            return None

        # The date must occur somewhere in the source document.
        if not g.date_is_grounded(parsed):
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-not-in-document",
                (
                    f"{label}: {parsed} does not appear anywhere in "
                    "the document, so it was left empty."
                ),
                label,
            )
            return None

        # The supporting quote must specifically support the date.
        if not g.quote_backs_date(
            parsed,
            fact.quote,
        ):
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-quote-mismatch",
                (
                    f"{label}: {parsed} is not supported by the "
                    "quoted text, so it was left empty."
                ),
                label,
            )
            return None

        # The quote must actually be found in the source document.
        supported = g.support(
            label,
            fact.quote,
            fact.passage,
        )

        if not supported:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-unverified",
                (
                    f"{label}: the supporting quote could not be "
                    "located in the document, so the date was left empty."
                ),
                label,
            )
            return None

        return parsed.isoformat()

    start = scalar(
        draft.application_start_date,
        "Application start date",
    )

    end = scalar(
        draft.application_end_date,
        "Application end date",
    )

    late = scalar(
        draft.late_fee_end_date,
        "Late-fee application end date",
    )

    if start is None and tba_hint:
        g.issue(
            "info",
            "start-tba",
            (
                "The application start date is listed as "
                "'to be announced' in the document."
            ),
            "Application start date",
        )

    if late:
        g.issue(
            "info",
            "late-fee-deadline",
            (
                f"Extended (late-fee) application deadline: {late}. "
                "Only the regular deadline is stored."
            ),
            "Application end date",
        )

    if start and end and start > end:
        g.issue(
            "error",
            "date-order",
            "Application start date is after the application end date.",
            "Application end date",
        )

    ranges: list[ExamDateRange] = []
    seen: set[tuple[str, str]] = set()

    for item in draft.exam_dates:
        a = iso(item.start_date)
        b = iso(item.end_date)

        label = (
            f"Exam date ({item.label})"
            if item.label
            else "Exam date"
        )

        if a is None or b is None:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-unparsed",
                (
                    f"{label}: '{item.start_date} â€“ "
                    f"{item.end_date}' is not a valid date range."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        if a > b:
            a, b = b, a

            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-swapped",
                (
                    f"{label}: start and end were reversed in the "
                    "extraction and have been swapped."
                ),
                label,
            )

        # Both endpoints must exist in the document.
        missing = [
            d
            for d in (a, b)
            if not g.date_is_grounded(d)
        ]

        if missing:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-not-in-document",
                (
                    f"{label}: {', '.join(str(m) for m in missing)} "
                    "does not appear in the document; range dropped."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        # The quoted evidence must support both endpoints.
        if not g.quote_backs_date(
            a,
            item.quote,
        ):
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-quote-mismatch",
                (
                    f"{label}: start date {a} is not supported by "
                    "the quoted text; range dropped."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        if not g.quote_backs_date(
            b,
            item.quote,
        ):
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-quote-mismatch",
                (
                    f"{label}: end date {b} is not supported by "
                    "the quoted text; range dropped."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        # The quote itself must exist in the source.
        supported = g.support(
            label,
            item.quote,
            item.passage,
        )

        if not supported:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-unverified",
                (
                    f"{label}: the supporting quote could not be "
                    "located in the document; range dropped."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        key = (
            a.isoformat(),
            b.isoformat(),
        )

        if key in seen:
            continue

        seen.add(key)

        ranges.append(
            ExamDateRange(
                start_date=key[0],
                end_date=key[1],
            )
        )

    ranges.sort(
        key=lambda r: r.start_date
    )

    if end and ranges and ranges[0].start_date < end:
        g.issue(
            "warning",
            "exam-before-deadline",
            (
                "An exam date falls before the application end "
                "date; check the dates."
            ),
            "Exam dates",
        )

    return {
        "application_start_date": start,
        "application_end_date": end,
        "exam_dates": ranges,
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Eligibility
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _is_general(limit: AgeLimit) -> bool:
    """
    True for the limit that applies to every candidate.

    A concession for a category ("up to five years for SC/ST") is not a
    competing value of the same limit, so it must not become a date-of-birth
    rule or a "conflict". The category is recognised from `applies_to`, and
    also from the quoted sentence, because models often leave `applies_to`
    empty for concessions.
    """

    applies = (limit.applies_to or "").strip()

    if applies:
        if _CATEGORY_WORDS.search(applies):
            return False

        if _GENERAL_WORDS.search(applies):
            return True

    quote = limit.quote or ""

    if _RELAXATION_WORDS.search(quote) and _CATEGORY_WORDS.search(quote):
        return False

    return True


def _expand_higher(
    levels: list[str],
    evidence_text: str,
) -> list[str]:
    """
    '...or a higher degree is also eligible'
    -> include all higher levels.
    """

    if not re.search(
        r"higher (degree|qualification)|degree higher|higher than",
        evidence_text,
        re.I,
    ):
        return levels

    known = [
        lvl
        for lvl in levels
        if lvl in _LEVEL_ORDER
    ]

    if not known:
        return levels

    lowest = min(
        _LEVEL_ORDER.index(lvl)
        for lvl in known
    )

    return [
        *levels,
        *[
            lvl
            for lvl in _LEVEL_ORDER[lowest:]
            if lvl not in levels
        ],
    ]


def _nationality_backs_quote(
    country: str,
    quote: str | None,
) -> bool:
    """
    Check whether a canonical country name is supported by nationality
    wording in the evidence.

    Handles both country names and common demonyms, for example:

        India  -> Indian
        Nepal  -> Nepali / Nepalese
        Bhutan -> Bhutanese

    IMPORTANT:
    Missing quote is NOT considered sufficient evidence.
    """

    if not country:
        return False

    if not quote:
        return False

    normalized_quote = quote.lower()

    demonyms = {
        "india": (
            "india",
            "indian",
        ),
        "nepal": (
            "nepal",
            "nepali",
            "nepalese",
        ),
        "bhutan": (
            "bhutan",
            "bhutanese",
        ),
        "bangladesh": (
            "bangladesh",
            "bangladeshi",
        ),
        "sri lanka": (
            "sri lanka",
            "sri lankan",
        ),
        "pakistan": (
            "pakistan",
            "pakistani",
        ),
        "united states": (
            "united states",
            "american",
        ),
        "united kingdom": (
            "united kingdom",
            "british",
        ),
    }

    accepted = demonyms.get(
        country.lower(),
        (country.lower(),),
    )

    return any(
        re.search(
            rf"\b{re.escape(term)}\b",
            normalized_quote,
        )
        for term in accepted
    )


def _age_limit_backs_quote(
    limit: AgeLimit,
    g: Grounder,
    quote: str | None = None,
) -> bool:
    """
    Verify that the age and, when present, the reference date are
    represented in the supporting quote.

    `quote` overrides limit.quote when a quote had to be recovered from the
    document text.
    """

    quote = limit.quote if quote is None else quote

    if not quote:
        return False

    # The age itself must be explicitly present ("32 years", "age of 32",
    # "18-32 years"), as a whole number, in a sentence about years or age.
    if not re.search(
        rf"(?<!\d){re.escape(str(limit.years))}(?!\d)",
        quote,
    ):
        return False

    if not re.search(
        r"\b(?:years?|yrs?|age)\b",
        quote,
        re.IGNORECASE,
    ):
        return False

    # If an "as on" date was extracted, the same quote must support it.
    if limit.as_on_date:
        reference_date = iso(
            limit.as_on_date
        )

        if reference_date is None:
            return False

        if not g.quote_backs_date(
            reference_date,
            quote,
        ):
            return False

    return True


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Quote helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _passage_blocks(context_text: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
    """Split the rendered context pack back into (passage id, text)."""

    tags = list(_PASSAGE_TAG.finditer(context_text))

    blocks = []

    for index, tag in enumerate(tags):
        end = (
            tags[index + 1].start()
            if index + 1 < len(tags)
            else len(context_text)
        )

        blocks.append((tag.group(1), context_text[tag.end():end]))

    return blocks


def _recover_sentence(
    context_text: str,
    predicate,
) -> tuple[str, str] | None:
    """
    First sentence of the retrieved document text that satisfies
    `predicate`, with the id of the passage it is in.

    The sentence is copied from the document itself, so it can always be
    located again by the grounder.
    """

    for passage_id, text in _passage_blocks(context_text):
        for part in re.split(r"(?<=[.;:])\s+|\n+", text):
            sentence = part.strip()

            if len(sentence) < 12:
                continue

            if predicate(sentence):
                return sentence[:400], passage_id

    return None


def _resolve_quote(
    g: Grounder,
    quote: str | None,
    passage: str | None,
    context_text: str,
    predicate,
) -> tuple[str | None, str | None, bool]:
    """
    Keep the model's quote when it can be found in the document.

    Small models often return no quote, or one that is not verbatim. In that
    case the sentence of the retrieved text that matches the claim is used
    instead, and the reviewer is told. Returns (quote, passage, recovered).
    """

    if quote and locate_quote(quote, passage, g.passages) is not None:
        return quote, passage, False

    found = _recover_sentence(context_text, predicate)

    if found is None:
        return quote, passage, False

    return found[0], found[1], True


def _origin_only_countries(
    quote: str | None,
    countries: list[str],
) -> tuple[set[str], str | None]:
    """
    Countries that appear only in the "persons of Indian origin who migrated
    from ..." part of the nationality sentence, with that part of the
    sentence. India itself is never excluded.
    """

    if not quote:
        return set(), None

    match = _ORIGIN_START.search(quote)

    if match is None:
        return set(), None

    before = quote[: match.start()].lower()
    after = quote[match.start():].lower()

    excluded = {
        country
        for country in countries
        if country != "India"
        and country.lower() in after
        and country.lower() not in before
    }

    if not excluded:
        return set(), None

    return excluded, " ".join(quote[match.start():].split())[:240]


def _mentions_age(sentence: str, years: int) -> bool:
    if _RELAXATION_WORDS.search(sentence) and _CATEGORY_WORDS.search(sentence):
        return False

    return bool(
        re.search(rf"(?<!\d){years}(?!\d)", sentence)
        and re.search(r"\b(?:years?|yrs?|age)\b", sentence, re.IGNORECASE)
    )


def _level_backs_quote(level: str, quote: str | None) -> bool:
    """True when the quote talks about this educational level."""

    if not quote:
        return False

    pattern = _LEVEL_PATTERNS.get(level)

    if pattern is None or not pattern.search(quote):
        return False

    # "degree" alone also appears in "Master's degree"; for Bachelor's it
    # only counts when no higher level is mentioned.
    if (
        level == "Bachelor's"
        and not _BACHELOR_SPECIFIC.search(quote)
        and _HIGHER_WORDS.search(quote)
    ):
        return False

    return True


def _digits(text: str | None) -> set[str]:
    tokens = {
        str(int(number))
        for number in re.findall(r"\d+", text or "")
    }

    for word, digit in _NUMBER_WORDS.items():
        if re.search(rf"\b{word}\b", (text or "").lower()):
            tokens.add(digit)

    return tokens


def _numbers_supported(text: str | None, quote: str | None) -> bool:
    """
    Every number in an informational statement must occur in its quote.

    A summary ("born not earlier than 02.08.1996") is worded differently
    from the notice, so word overlap is a poor test for it; the numbers are
    what must not be invented.
    """

    return _digits(text) <= _digits(quote)


def _short(text: str, width: int = 50) -> str:
    if len(text) <= width:
        return text

    cut = text[:width].rsplit(" ", 1)[0] or text[:width]

    return cut + "â€¦"


def compile_eligibility(
    draft: EligibilityDraft,
    g: Grounder,
    context_text: str,
) -> tuple[
    EligibilityInformation,
    EligibilityRulesData,
]:
    rules: list[EligibilityRuleData] = []
    children: list[EligibilityRuleGroupData] = []
    other: list[str] = []

    # ---- age -> date of birth ------------------------------------------

    min_age = None
    max_age = None

    general: dict[str, list[AgeLimit]] = {
        "minimum": [],
        "maximum": [],
    }

    for limit in draft.age_limits:
        if limit.years <= 0 or limit.years > 120:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "age-invalid",
                (
                    f"Ignored implausible age limit: "
                    f"{limit.years} years."
                ),
                "Age limit",
            )
            continue

        if _is_general(limit):
            general[limit.kind].append(limit)
        else:
            who = (
                (limit.applies_to or "").strip()
                or _short((limit.quote or "").strip(), 70)
                or "a category of candidates"
            )

            text = (
                f"Age {limit.kind} {limit.years} years for {who}"
                + (
                    f" (as on {limit.as_on_date})"
                    if limit.as_on_date
                    else ""
                )
            )

            if text not in other:
                other.append(text)

            g.support(
                f"Age limit ({who})",
                limit.quote,
                limit.passage,
                required=False,
            )

    dob_rules: list[EligibilityRuleData] = []

    # One "as on" date for the whole notice, used when the model did not
    # report one for a limit.
    default_reference = find_age_reference_date(context_text)

    for kind, limits in general.items():
        if not limits:
            continue

        distinct = {
            (
                l.years,
                l.as_on_date,
            )
            for l in limits
        }

        resolved = []

        for limit in limits:
            quote, passage, recovered = _resolve_quote(
                g,
                limit.quote,
                limit.passage,
                context_text,
                lambda sentence, years=limit.years: _mentions_age(
                    sentence, years
                ),
            )

            resolved.append((limit, quote, passage, recovered))

        # Prefer an age limit whose value and reference date are
        # actually supported by its quoted evidence.
        supported_limits = [
            item
            for item in resolved
            if _age_limit_backs_quote(
                item[0],
                g,
                item[1],
            )
        ]

        if not supported_limits:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "age-value-quote-mismatch",
                (
                    f"Age limit ({kind}): none of the extracted "
                    "age/reference-date values are fully supported "
                    "by their quoted text; no date-of-birth rule "
                    "was created."
                ),
                f"Age limit ({kind})",
            )
            continue

        chosen, quote, passage, recovered = supported_limits[0]

        if len(distinct) > 1:
            g.conflict(
                f"Age limit ({kind})",
                [
                    ConflictOption(
                        value=(
                            f"{l.years} years"
                            + (
                                f" as on {l.as_on_date}"
                                if l.as_on_date
                                else ""
                            )
                        ),
                        source=(l.quote or "")[:160],
                        pages=(
                            g.passages[l.passage].pages
                            if l.passage in g.passages
                            else []
                        ),
                    )
                    for l in limits
                ],
                f"{chosen.years} years",
                (
                    "Provisionally using the first general limit "
                    "found; verify against the document."
                ),
            )

        label = f"Age limit ({kind})"

        # "must not have attained 32" means the last eligible age is 31.
        exclusive = is_exclusive(kind, quote)

        shown = chosen.years

        if exclusive:
            shown = chosen.years - 1 if kind == "maximum" else chosen.years + 1

        if kind == "minimum":
            min_age = shown
        else:
            max_age = shown

        # The supporting quote must actually be located before
        # considering the age fact authoritative.
        supported = g.support(
            label,
            quote,
            passage,
        )

        if not supported:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "age-unverified",
                (
                    f"{label}: the supporting quote could not be "
                    "located in the document; no date-of-birth "
                    "rule was created."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        if recovered:
            g.issue(
                "info",
                "quote-recovered",
                (
                    f"{label}: the model gave no usable quote, so the "
                    "matching sentence from the document was used."
                ),
                label,
            )

        if exclusive:
            g.issue(
                "info",
                "age-exclusive",
                (
                    f"{label}: the notice says candidates must not have "
                    f"attained {chosen.years} years, so the last eligible "
                    f"age is {shown}."
                    if kind == "maximum"
                    else (
                        f"{label}: the notice says candidates must be "
                        f"older than {chosen.years}, so the first eligible "
                        f"age is {shown}."
                    )
                ),
                label,
            )

        ref = iso(
            chosen.as_on_date
        )

        if ref is None and default_reference is not None:
            ref = default_reference

            g.issue(
                "info",
                "age-reference-from-notice",
                (
                    f"{label}: the 'as on' date {ref} was taken from "
                    "the notification text."
                ),
                label,
            )

        if ref is None:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "age-no-reference-date",
                (
                    f"The {kind} age of {chosen.years} years has "
                    "no 'as on' date in the document, so no "
                    "date-of-birth rule was created."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        if not g.date_is_grounded(ref):
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "date-not-in-document",
                (
                    f"{label}: reference date {ref} is not in "
                    "the document."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        operator, boundary = dob_boundary(
            kind,
            chosen.years,
            ref,
            exclusive=exclusive,
        )

        dob_rules.append(
            EligibilityRuleData(
                attribute="Date of Birth",
                operator=operator,
                value=boundary.isoformat(),
            )
        )

    # ---- birth dates written out in the notice -----------------------
    #
    # "born not earlier than 2nd August 1993 and not later than
    # 1st August 2004" is authoritative; when present it replaces the
    # boundary calculated from the age.

    explicit = parse_explicit_dob_limits(
        context_text,
        exclude=_CATEGORY_WORDS,
    )

    for key, operator, words in (
        ("earliest", ">=", "not earlier than"),
        ("latest", "<=", "not later than"),
    ):
        found = explicit[key]

        if found is None:
            continue

        found_date, snippet = found

        if not g.date_is_grounded(found_date):
            continue

        if not g.support(
            f"Date of birth ({words})",
            snippet,
            None,
            required=False,
        ):
            continue

        replaced = [r for r in dob_rules if r.operator == operator]

        dob_rules = [r for r in dob_rules if r.operator != operator]

        if replaced and replaced[0].value != found_date.isoformat():
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "dob-from-notice",
                (
                    f"The notice states the date of birth limit "
                    f"({words} {found_date}) outright; it was used "
                    f"instead of the value calculated from the age "
                    f"limit ({replaced[0].value})."
                ),
                "Date of birth",
            )

        dob_rules.append(
            EligibilityRuleData(
                attribute="Date of Birth",
                operator=operator,
                value=found_date.isoformat(),
            )
        )

    if explicit.get("varies") and (explicit["earliest"] or explicit["latest"]):
        g.issue(
            "warning",
            "dob-varies-by-post",
            (
                "The notice gives different birth-date limits for "
                "different posts. The most generous limit was used for "
                "the exam-level rule; check the post-wise limits."
            ),
            "Date of birth",
        )

    rules.extend(dob_rules)

    if draft.has_no_age_limit and not draft.age_limits:
        other.append("No age limit")

    # ---- qualifications: OR of accepted levels -----------------------

    levels: list[str] = []
    fields: list[str] = []
    quals_text = ""

    for q in draft.qualifications:
        label = f"Qualification: {q.level}"

        # Use the model's quote when it is in the document; otherwise the
        # sentence of the document that talks about this level.
        quote, passage, recovered = _resolve_quote(
            g,
            q.quote,
            q.passage,
            context_text,
            lambda sentence, level=q.level: _level_backs_quote(
                level, sentence
            ),
        )

        # The quote must exist in the source document.
        quote_found = g.support(
            label,
            quote,
            passage,
            required=False,
        )

        # The extracted qualification must also agree with its evidence.
        # "Bachelor's" is backed by "a degree of a recognised university",
        # so the level is checked by meaning as well as by wording.
        value_supported = g.value_backs_quote(
            q.level,
            quote,
        ) or _level_backs_quote(q.level, quote)

        if not value_supported:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "value-quote-mismatch",
                (
                    f"{label}: the extracted qualification is "
                    "not supported by the quoted text; it was ignored."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        if not quote_found:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "qualification-unverified",
                (
                    f"{label}: the supporting quote could not be "
                    "located in the document; it was ignored."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        if recovered:
            g.issue(
                "info",
                "quote-recovered",
                (
                    f"{label}: the model gave no usable quote, so the "
                    "matching sentence from the document was used."
                ),
                label,
            )

        if q.level not in levels:
            levels.append(q.level)

        if q.field and q.field not in fields:
            fields.append(q.field)

        quals_text += " " + (
            quote or ""
        )

    levels = _expand_higher(
        levels,
        quals_text + " " + context_text,
    )

    if levels:
        children.append(
            EligibilityRuleGroupData(
                logical_operator="OR",
                rules=[
                    EligibilityRuleData(
                        attribute="Educational Qualification",
                        operator="=",
                        value=level,
                    )
                    for level in levels
                ],
            )
        )

    # ---- scores --------------------------------------------------------

    score_rule_created = False

    if draft.minimum_percentage is not None:
        percentage_value = _num(
            draft.minimum_percentage
        )

        score_supported = g.support(
            "Minimum percentage",
            draft.score_quote,
            draft.score_passage,
        )

        if (
            score_supported
            and g.value_backs_quote(
                percentage_value,
                draft.score_quote,
            )
        ):
            rules.append(
                EligibilityRuleData(
                    attribute="Percentage",
                    operator=">=",
                    value=percentage_value,
                )
            )

            score_rule_created = True
        elif score_supported:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "value-quote-mismatch",
                (
                    f"Minimum percentage '{percentage_value}' "
                    "is not supported by the quoted text; "
                    "it was ignored."
                ),
                "Minimum percentage",
            )

    if draft.minimum_cgpa is not None:
        cgpa_value = _num(
            draft.minimum_cgpa
        )

        score_supported = g.support(
            "Minimum CGPA",
            draft.score_quote,
            draft.score_passage,
        )

        if (
            score_supported
            and g.value_backs_quote(
                cgpa_value,
                draft.score_quote,
            )
        ):
            rules.append(
                EligibilityRuleData(
                    attribute="CGPA",
                    operator=">=",
                    value=cgpa_value,
                )
            )

            score_rule_created = True
        elif score_supported:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                "value-quote-mismatch",
                (
                    f"Minimum CGPA '{cgpa_value}' is not "
                    "supported by the quoted text; it was ignored."
                ),
                "Minimum CGPA",
            )

    # ---- nationality: OR of countries -------------------------------

    countries: list[str] = []

    # Nationality is executable eligibility data, so the supporting
    # quote must actually exist in the source document. Nothing is
    # checked (or reported) when the notice names no nationality.
    if draft.nationalities:
        nat_quote, nat_passage, nat_recovered = _resolve_quote(
            g,
            draft.nationality_quote,
            draft.nationality_passage,
            context_text,
            _NATIONALITY_SENTENCE.search,
        )

        nationality_supported = g.support(
            "Nationality",
            nat_quote,
            nat_passage,
        )

        if nationality_supported and nat_recovered:
            g.issue(
                "info",
                "quote-recovered",
                (
                    "Nationality: the model gave no usable quote, so "
                    "the matching sentence from the document was used."
                ),
                "Nationality",
            )

        if nationality_supported:
            canonical = [
                canonical_country(name) for name in draft.nationalities
            ]

            origin_only, origin_text = _origin_only_countries(
                nat_quote,
                [c for c in canonical if c],
            )

            if origin_text:
                note = f"Nationality: {origin_text}"

                if note not in other:
                    other.append(note)

            for name in draft.nationalities:
                country = canonical_country(name)

                if not country:
                    continue

                if country in origin_only:
                    continue

                # "Persons of Indian origin who migrated from ..." is a
                # condition, not a country. It cannot be compared with a
                # student's nationality, so it is shown to the reviewer
                # instead of becoming a rule that can never match.
                if len(country) > 40 or _FREE_TEXT_COUNTRY.search(country):
                    note = f"Nationality: {name.strip()}"

                    if note not in other:
                        other.append(note)

                    continue

                if not _nationality_backs_quote(
                    country,
                    nat_quote,
                ):
                    g.issue(
                        "warning",
                        "value-quote-mismatch",
                        (
                            f"Nationality '{country}' is not supported "
                            "by the quoted text; it was ignored."
                        ),
                        "Nationality",
                    )
                    continue

                if country not in countries:
                    countries.append(country)

            if countries:
                children.append(
                    EligibilityRuleGroupData(
                        logical_operator="OR",
                        rules=[
                            EligibilityRuleData(
                                attribute="Nationality",
                                operator="=",
                                value=country,
                            )
                            for country in countries
                        ],
                    )
                )

    # ---- work experience is informational ----------------------------

    #
    # The current eligibility evaluator only supports text equality
    # for Work Experience, so numeric experience requirements are not
    # converted into executable rules yet.

    work = draft.work_experience.value

    if work:
        work_label = "Work experience"

        quote_found = g.support(
            work_label,
            draft.work_experience.quote,
            draft.work_experience.passage,
            required=False,
        )

        # The statement is a summary, so its numbers (not its wording)
        # must be present in the quote.
        value_supported = _numbers_supported(
            work,
            draft.work_experience.quote,
        )

        if quote_found and value_supported:
            # Keep this informational until the evaluator supports
            # numeric work-experience comparisons.
            pass
        else:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                (
                    "value-quote-mismatch"
                    if not value_supported
                    else "work-experience-unverified"
                ),
                (
                    f"{work_label}: the extracted value is not "
                    "supported by the quoted text; it was ignored."
                ),
                work_label,
            )
            work = None

    # ---- other requirements -------------------------------------------

    for item in draft.other_requirements:
        text = item.text.strip()

        if not text:
            continue

        label = f"Requirement: {_short(text)}"

        quote_found = g.support(
            label,
            item.quote,
            item.passage,
            required=False,
        )

        # Informational statements are summaries: they are kept when the
        # quote is in the document and every number in them is in the quote.
        value_supported = _numbers_supported(
            text,
            item.quote,
        )

        if not quote_found or not value_supported:
            g.issue(
                "warning",
                (
                    "value-quote-mismatch"
                    if not value_supported
                    else "requirement-unverified"
                ),
                (
                    f"{label}: the extracted requirement is not "
                    "sufficiently supported by its quoted text; "
                    "it was ignored."
                ),
                label,
            )
            continue

        if text not in other:
            other.append(text)

    info = EligibilityInformation(
        minimum_age=min_age,
        maximum_age=max_age,
        educational_qualification=(
            ", ".join(levels)
            + (
                f" ({'; '.join(fields)})"
                if fields
                else ""
            )
            if levels
            else None
        ),
        nationality=", ".join(countries) or None,
        work_experience=work,
        other_requirements=other,
    )

    groups = []

    if rules or children:
        groups.append(
            EligibilityRuleGroupData(
                logical_operator="AND",
                rules=rules,
                child_groups=children,
            )
        )

    return (
        info,
        EligibilityRulesData(
            rule_groups=groups
        ),
    )


def _num(x: float) -> str:
    return (
        str(int(x))
        if float(x).is_integer()
        else str(x)
    )


========== FILE: app\ai\extraction\dates.py ==========

from __future__ import annotations

"""
Deterministic date finder.

Used to:
1. rank passages by how date-dense they are, and
2. verify that dates reported by the LLM actually occur in the document.

Day-first numeric dates are assumed for ambiguous numeric formats,
which matches the Indian convention used by NexStep.

Examples supported:

    6 February 2027
    February 6, 2027
    6th February 2027
    1st of August, 2025
    February 6 & 7, 2027
    6 & 7 February 2027
    February 6-8, 2027
    6-8 February 2027
    Sep 22 - Sep 30, 2026
    Aug 14, 2026 to Sep 21, 2026
    06/02/2027
    06-02-2027
    06.02.2027
    2027-02-06
"""

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date


_MONTHS = {
    "january": 1,
    "jan": 1,
    "february": 2,
    "feb": 2,
    "march": 3,
    "mar": 3,
    "april": 4,
    "apr": 4,
    "may": 5,
    "june": 6,
    "jun": 6,
    "july": 7,
    "jul": 7,
    "august": 8,
    "aug": 8,
    "september": 9,
    "sep": 9,
    "sept": 9,
    "october": 10,
    "oct": 10,
    "november": 11,
    "nov": 11,
    "december": 12,
    "dec": 12,
}


_MONTH = (
    r"(?:"
    r"january|february|march|april|may|june|july|august|"
    r"september|october|november|december|"
    r"jan|feb|mar|apr|jun|jul|aug|sept|sep|oct|nov|dec"
    r")"
)

_ORDINAL = r"(?:st|nd|rd|th)?"

_DAY = rf"(\d{{1,2}}){_ORDINAL}"
_DAY_NC = rf"\d{{1,2}}{_ORDINAL}"


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Month + day list
#
# Examples:
#   February 6 & 7, 2027
#   Feb 6, 7 and 8, 2027
#   February 6-7, 2027
#   February 6 to 8, 2027
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_MONTH_DAYLIST = re.compile(
    rf"\b({_MONTH})\s+"
    rf"({_DAY_NC}"
    rf"(?:\s*(?:,|&|and|to|-|â€“|â€”)\s*{_DAY_NC})*)"
    rf"\s*,?\s*(\d{{4}})\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Day list + month
#
# Examples:
#   6 & 7 February 2027
#   6-7 February, 2027
#   6th to 8th Feb 2027
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_DAYLIST_MONTH = re.compile(
    rf"\b("
    rf"{_DAY_NC}"
    rf"(?:\s*(?:,|&|and|to|-|â€“|â€”)\s*{_DAY_NC})*"
    rf")\s+(?:of\s+)?"
    rf"({_MONTH})\s*,?\s*(\d{{4}})\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Month-to-month ranges
#
# Examples:
#   Sep 22 - Sep 30, 2026
#   Aug 14, 2026 to Sep 21, 2026
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_MONTH_TO_MONTH = re.compile(
    rf"\b"
    rf"({_MONTH})\s+{_DAY}"
    rf"(?:\s*,?\s*(\d{{4}}))?"
    rf"\s*(?:to|-|â€“|â€”)\s*"
    rf"({_MONTH})\s+{_DAY}"
    rf"\s*,?\s*(\d{{4}})"
    rf"\b",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Numeric dates
#
# Indian/day-first interpretation:
#
#   06/02/2027 -> 6 February 2027
#   06-02-2027 -> 6 February 2027
#   06.02.2027 -> 6 February 2027
#
# Two-digit years are interpreted as 2000-2099.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_NUMERIC = re.compile(
    r"\b(\d{1,2})[/.\-](\d{1,2})[/.\-](\d{2}|\d{4})\b"
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# ISO dates
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_ISO = re.compile(
    r"\b(\d{4})-(\d{2})-(\d{2})\b"
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Range separators used when expanding day lists
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_RANGE_SEP = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:to)\b|[-â€“â€”]",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DateMention:
    value: date
    start: int
    end: int


def _month(token: str) -> int | None:
    return _MONTHS.get(
        token.lower().rstrip(".")
    )


def _safe(
    year: int,
    month: int,
    day: int,
) -> date | None:
    try:
        return date(year, month, day)
    except ValueError:
        return None


def _expand_days(daylist: str) -> list[int]:
    """
    Expand a day list.

    Examples:

        "6 & 7"  -> [6, 7]
        "6, 7, 8" -> [6, 7, 8]
        "6-8"    -> [6, 7, 8]
        "6 to 8" -> [6, 7, 8]
    """

    numbers = [
        int(number)
        for number in re.findall(
            r"\d{1,2}",
            daylist,
        )
    ]

    if (
        len(numbers) == 2
        and _RANGE_SEP.search(daylist)
        and numbers[0] < numbers[1] <= 31
    ):
        return list(
            range(
                numbers[0],
                numbers[1] + 1,
            )
        )

    return numbers


def _year(value: int) -> int:
    """
    Convert a two-digit year to the 2000s.
    """

    if value < 100:
        return 2000 + value

    return value


def find_dates(text: str) -> list[DateMention]:
    """
    Find all recognizable dates in text.

    Invalid calendar dates are silently ignored.
    """

    found: list[DateMention] = []

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Month followed by day list
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------

    for match in _MONTH_DAYLIST.finditer(text):
        month = _month(match.group(1))
        year = int(match.group(3))

        if month is None:
            continue

        for day in _expand_days(match.group(2)):
            parsed = _safe(
                year,
                month,
                day,
            )

            if parsed is not None:
                found.append(
                    DateMention(
                        parsed,
                        match.start(),
                        match.end(),
                    )
                )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Day list followed by month
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------

    for match in _DAYLIST_MONTH.finditer(text):
        month = _month(match.group(2))
        year = int(match.group(3))

        if month is None:
            continue

        for day in _expand_days(match.group(1)):
            parsed = _safe(
                year,
                month,
                day,
            )

            if parsed is not None:
                found.append(
                    DateMention(
                        parsed,
                        match.start(),
                        match.end(),
                    )
                )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Month-to-month ranges
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------

    for match in _MONTH_TO_MONTH.finditer(text):
        start_month_token = match.group(1)
        start_day = int(match.group(2))
        start_year = (
            int(match.group(3))
            if match.group(3)
            else int(match.group(6))
        )

        end_month_token = match.group(4)
        end_day = int(match.group(5))
        end_year = int(match.group(6))

        start_month = _month(start_month_token)
        end_month = _month(end_month_token)

        if start_month is not None:
            parsed = _safe(
                start_year,
                start_month,
                start_day,
            )

            if parsed is not None:
                found.append(
                    DateMention(
                        parsed,
                        match.start(),
                        match.end(),
                    )
                )

        if end_month is not None:
            parsed = _safe(
                end_year,
                end_month,
                end_day,
            )

            if parsed is not None:
                found.append(
                    DateMention(
                        parsed,
                        match.start(),
                        match.end(),
                    )
                )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # ISO
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------

    for match in _ISO.finditer(text):
        parsed = _safe(
            int(match.group(1)),
            int(match.group(2)),
            int(match.group(3)),
        )

        if parsed is not None:
            found.append(
                DateMention(
                    parsed,
                    match.start(),
                    match.end(),
                )
            )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Numeric day-first dates
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------

    for match in _NUMERIC.finditer(text):
        parsed = _safe(
            _year(int(match.group(3))),
            int(match.group(2)),
            int(match.group(1)),
        )

        if parsed is not None:
            found.append(
                DateMention(
                    parsed,
                    match.start(),
                    match.end(),
                )
            )

    return found


def date_set(text: str) -> set[date]:
    """
    Return unique dates found in text.
    """

    return {
        mention.value
        for mention in find_dates(text)
    }


def count_dates(text: str) -> int:
    """
    Return the number of date mentions found in text.
    """

    return len(
        find_dates(text)
    )

========== FILE: app\ai\extraction\facts.py ==========

from __future__ import annotations

"""
What the LLM is asked to return.

The schemas are deliberately flat and simple (a small model must be able
to fill them in). The model reports FACTS, each with the passage id and a
verbatim quote it came from. Turning facts into eligibility rules, and
age limits into date-of-birth boundaries, is done in Python (compiler.py),
because that logic is deterministic and models are unreliable at it.

Important grounding rule:

Every extracted examination date MUST include:
    - the passage id where the date was found
    - a short verbatim quote from that passage

This is enforced by the schema instead of relying only on the prompt.
"""

from typing import Any, Literal

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field


QualLevel = Literal[
    "10th",
    "12th / Senior Secondary",
    "Diploma",
    "Bachelor's",
    "Master's",
    "PhD",
    "Other",
]


class Fact(BaseModel):
    value: str | None = None
    quote: str | None = None
    passage: str | None = None


class IdentityDraft(BaseModel):
    exam_name: Fact
    conducting_body: Fact
    release_date: Fact


class DateRangeFact(BaseModel):
    """
    One examination date/range.

    Unlike generic Fact objects, quote and passage are REQUIRED here.

    If the model cannot provide source evidence for an examination date,
    it should NOT create a DateRangeFact for that date.
    """

    label: str | None = None

    start_date: str
    end_date: str

    quote: str = Field(
        min_length=1,
        description=(
            "Short verbatim quote copied from the supplied passage. "
            "The quote must contain the examination date or date range."
        ),
    )

    passage: str = Field(
        min_length=1,
        description=(
            "ID of the supplied passage containing the quoted examination date."
        ),
    )


class DatesDraft(BaseModel):
    application_start_date: Fact
    application_end_date: Fact
    late_fee_end_date: Fact
    exam_dates: list[DateRangeFact] = Field(default_factory=list)


class AgeLimit(BaseModel):
    kind: Literal["minimum", "maximum"]
    years: int
    applies_to: str | None = None
    as_on_date: str | None = None
    quote: str | None = None
    passage: str | None = None


class QualificationFact(BaseModel):
    level: QualLevel
    field: str | None = None
    quote: str | None = None
    passage: str | None = None


class TextItem(BaseModel):
    text: str
    quote: str | None = None
    passage: str | None = None


class EligibilityDraft(BaseModel):
    has_no_age_limit: bool = False

    age_limits: list[AgeLimit] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )

    qualifications: list[QualificationFact] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )

    minimum_percentage: float | None = None
    minimum_cgpa: float | None = None

    score_quote: str | None = None
    score_passage: str | None = None

    nationalities: list[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )

    nationality_quote: str | None = None
    nationality_passage: str | None = None

    work_experience: Fact

    other_requirements: list[TextItem] = Field(
        default_factory=list
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# JSON schema helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def inline_schema(model: type[BaseModel]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """
    JSON schema with every $ref inlined.

    Providers differ in how well they support $defs/$ref in structured
    output. A self-contained schema works more reliably across providers.
    """

    schema = model.model_json_schema()

    defs = schema.pop("$defs", {})

    def resolve(node: Any) -> Any:
        if isinstance(node, dict):

            if "$ref" in node:
                name = node["$ref"].rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
                return resolve(dict(defs[name]))

            return {
                key: resolve(value)
                for key, value in node.items()
                if key not in {"title", "default"}
            }

        if isinstance(node, list):
            return [resolve(item) for item in node]

        return node

    return resolve(schema)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Prompts
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

SYSTEM_PROMPT = (
    "You extract facts from an official examination notification. "
    "Use ONLY the supplied passages. Never use outside knowledge and never "
    "guess. If a fact is not stated, use null (or an empty list). "
    "Every extracted fact must be traceable to the supplied passages. "
    "Every fact that has a value must include the passage id and a short "
    "VERBATIM quote copied from that passage. "
    "For examination dates this requirement is mandatory: never return an "
    "exam_dates item without both passage and quote. If you cannot provide "
    "a source quote for an examination date, do not return that date as an "
    "exam_dates item. "
    "Return one JSON object and nothing else."
)


_COMMON_RULES = """\
General rules:
- Dates: YYYY-MM-DD.
- If the passage says a date is 'to be announced' or gives no date, use null.
- Ignore values marked as superseded, cancelled or replaced.
- Do not copy a date unless it is written in a supplied passage.
- Never invent a passage id.
- Never invent a quotation.
"""


IDENTITY_TASK = """\
Task: identify the examination.

- exam_name: the official name of the examination including the year/edition
  (e.g. 'GATE 2027').
- conducting_body: the organisation that conducts or organises it.
- release_date: the date this notification/brochure was issued or last revised,
  if stated.
""" + _COMMON_RULES


DATES_TASK = """\
Task: extract the schedule.

- application_start_date: when online registration/applications open
  (regular period).
- application_end_date: last date to apply in the REGULAR period.
- late_fee_end_date: last date to apply in the extended / late-fee period,
  if any.

- exam_dates: every actual examination date stated in the supplied passages.

For exam_dates:

1. A consecutive multi-day examination must be represented as one range.
2. A single examination day must use the same date for start_date and end_date.
3. Separate sessions, papers, stages, weeks, or non-consecutive examination
   periods should be represented as separate items when the notification
   distinguishes them.
4. Do NOT include dates of results, answer keys, admit cards, application
   periods, interviews, counselling, or other non-examination events.
5. Every exam_dates item MUST contain:
   - start_date
   - end_date
   - passage
   - quote
6. The quote MUST be copied VERBATIM from the supplied passage.
7. The quote MUST contain the examination date or date range supporting the
   extracted start_date/end_date.
8. The passage MUST be the exact passage id containing that quote.
9. Never create an exam_dates item with quote=null, quote="", passage=null,
   or passage="".
10. If an examination date is visible but you cannot provide both a valid
    passage id and a verbatim supporting quote, OMIT that exam_dates item
    instead of guessing.

Example of the required structure:

{
  "label": "Civil Services Preliminary Examination",
  "start_date": "2025-05-25",
  "end_date": "2025-05-25",
  "quote": "The Civil Services (Preliminary) Examination, 2025 will be held on 25.05.2025.",
  "passage": "P12"
}

The quote above is only an example of the format. Do not copy the example
as evidence. Use only quotes from the supplied passages.
""" + _COMMON_RULES


ELIGIBILITY_TASK = """\
Task: extract who may apply.

Every item below needs "passage" (the id of the passage it came from) and
"quote" (a short sentence copied word for word from that passage). An item
without a quote cannot be used, so leave it out instead of guessing.

- age_limits: one item per age limit.
  kind is 'minimum' or 'maximum'.
  years is the number exactly as written in the notice. Copy it; do not
  add or subtract anything. ("must not have attained the age of 32 years"
  -> years = 32.)
  as_on_date: the date age is counted on, only if the passage states it.
  applies_to: leave null ONLY for the limit that applies to every
  candidate. Any limit or concession that applies to a particular group
  (SC, ST, OBC, EWS, persons with disabilities, ex-servicemen, defence
  personnel, government servants, women, ...) must name that group in
  applies_to. If a concession is written as "up to five years in the case
  of ...", report the resulting age for that group (general limit plus the
  concession) with applies_to set to the group.

- qualifications: each accepted educational level (alternatives).
  level must be one of the allowed values.
  Put the subject area in field.
  quote: the sentence that names the qualification.

- minimum_percentage / minimum_cgpa:
  only if a minimum score is required.
  score_quote / score_passage: the sentence stating it.

- nationalities:
  names of countries only, one per entry (e.g. ['India', 'Nepal',
  'Bhutan']). Do not put conditions or descriptions here: "persons of
  Indian origin who migrated from ...", refugee conditions and similar
  wording belong in other_requirements. Empty if not restricted.
  nationality_quote / nationality_passage: the sentence that lists who
  may apply.

- work_experience:
  required work experience, if any.

- other_requirements:
  other conditions worth showing an administrator
  (attempt limits, year of study, category conditions, dates of birth
  written out in the notice, etc.). Keep each item short, and keep every
  number exactly as the notice writes it.
""" + _COMMON_RULES


def build_messages(
    task: str,
    context: str,
    feedback: str | None,
) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
    parts = [task]

    if feedback:
        parts.append(
            "Administrator feedback about a previous attempt "
            "(guidance only, NOT evidence; verify everything against "
            "the passages):\n"
            + feedback.strip()
        )

    parts.append(
        "PASSAGES:\n" + context
    )

    return [
        {
            "role": "system",
            "content": SYSTEM_PROMPT,
        },
        {
            "role": "user",
            "content": "\n\n".join(parts),
        },
    ]


========== FILE: app\ai\extraction\grounding.py ==========

from __future__ import annotations

"""
Grounding: nothing the model says is trusted until it is found in the
source document.

Quotes are located in passages with page numbers and section.
Dates must exist somewhere in the document.
Extracted values can additionally be checked against their supporting quote.
"""

import re
import unicodedata
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import date

from app.ai.aggregated_extraction_result import (
    ConflictOption,
    ExtractionConflict,
    ExtractionIssue,
)
from app.ai.extraction.dates import date_set, find_dates
from app.ai.extraction.structure import Passage
from app.ai.extraction_evidence import ExtractionEvidence


def _norm(text: str) -> str:
    text = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or "").lower()

    text = re.sub(r"[\u2010-\u2015\-]+", "-", text)

    text = re.sub(
        r"""["'â€œâ€â€˜â€™`â€¢*_#\[\]|]+""",
        " ",
        text,
    )

    text = re.sub(
        r"[^\w\s\-/.,:;()&%â‚¹]",
        " ",
        text,
    )

    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text).strip()


def _words(text: str) -> list[str]:
    return re.findall(r"\w+", _norm(text))


@dataclass(slots=True)
class Location:
    passage: Passage
    exact: bool


def locate_quote(
    quote: str | None,
    cited: str | None,
    passages: dict[str, Passage],
) -> Location | None:
    """Find the passage a quote comes from, checking the cited passage first."""

    if not quote or len(_norm(quote)) < 4:
        return None

    needle = _norm(quote)

    order = (
        ([cited] if cited in passages else [])
        + [pid for pid in passages if pid != cited]
    )

    # Exact normalized match.
    for pid in order:
        if needle in _norm(passages[pid].text):
